. That this is the case is a property of our specific system of structural laws and handled by the above Prop.
D.72
. This would obviously be violated, e.g., if we added a structural law like
\ell{:}H{\mathsf{.}}Q\equiv\alpha^{\prime}{:}H^{\prime}{\mathsf{.}}Q^{\prime}
where
\alpha^{\prime}\neq\ell
,
H^{\prime}\neq H
,
E^{\prime}\not\equiv{{\mathsf{0}}}
or
Q^{\prime}\not\equiv Q
.

As Examples
8
and
9
show,
a prefix
\ell{:}H{\mathsf{.}}Q
for
\ell\in\mathcal{R}
can very well be coherent even if
\ell\not\in H
. Here we note that for clock prefixes, the blocking set is only constrained by the policy.

Proposition D.78
.

If
Q
is coherent for
\pi
and
\sigma\in\mathcal{C}
is a clock, then the prefix expression
\sigma{:}H{\mathsf{.}}Q
is coherent for
\pi
, too, if
H\subseteq\{\beta\mid\beta\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\;\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}{}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip-6.75003pt\raisebox{6.22778pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt\scriptsize$$\hskip 3.00003pt}\hskip 0.74997pt\raisebox{0.22223pt}{\scalebox{.5}[.5]{$\succ$}}\;$}\sigma\in\pi\}
.

Proof D.79
.

The argument runs exactly as in case of Prop.
D.76
. However, we do not need to require condition
\sigma\in H
because if
\alpha_{i}=\sigma
then
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}
,
Q_{1}\equiv Q_{2}
and
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\not\subseteq\mathcal{R}
. So, no reconvergence is required.
Again, we observe that the only immediate derivative of
\sigma{:}H{\mathsf{.}}Q
is
Q
which is coherent for
\pi
by assumption.

D.2
Summation

Proposition D.80
.

Let
P_{1}\Vdash\pi_{1}
and
P_{2}\Vdash\pi_{2}
be coherent and for all pairs of initial transitions

P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}P_{1}\text{ and }P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}P_{2}
we have
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
as well as
\alpha_{1}\in H_{2}
or
\alpha_{2}\in H_{1}
.
Then
P_{1}+P_{2}
is coherent for
\pi
if
\pi_{1}\subseteq\pi
and
\pi_{2}\subseteq\pi
.

Proof D.81
.

Let
P=Q+R
be given with
Q
and
R
locally coherent for
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
, respectively and the rest as in the statement of the proposition. Every transition of
P
is either a transition of
Q
or of
R
via rules
(\textit{Sum}_{i})
. Conformance to
\pi
directly follows from the same property of
Q
or
R
, respectively. The proof of coherence Def.
12
is straightforward, exploiting that two competing but non-interfering transitions must either be both from
Q
or both from
R
. More precisely, suppose

P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}P_{1}\text{ and }P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}P_{2}
with
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
or
P_{1}\not\equiv P_{2}
or
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha_{i}\not\in H_{3-i}
.
Also, we assume that
\alpha_{1}{:}H_{1}[F_{1}]
and
\alpha_{2}{:}H_{2}[F_{2}]
are interference-free.
Now if the two transitions of
P
are by
(\textit{Sum}_{1})
from
Q
or by
(\textit{Sum}_{2})
from
R
, then
\alpha_{1}\in\textsf{iA}(Q)
and
\alpha_{2}\in\textsf{iA}(R)
. But then by assumption,
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{1}\in H_{2}
or
\alpha_{2}\in H_{1}
, which implies that
\alpha_{1}{:}H_{1}[F_{1}]
and
\alpha_{2}{:}H_{2}[E_{2}]
are not interference-free. This means, to prove coherence Def.
12
(2) we may assume that both transitions of
P
are either by
(\textit{Sum}_{1})
from
Q
or both by
(\textit{Sum}_{2})
from
R
.
Suppose both reductions of
P=Q+R
are generated by rule
(\textit{Sum}_{1})
, i.e.,

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\text{ and }Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}
where the transitions generated by
(\textit{Sum}_{1})
are

Q+R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\text{ and }Q+R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}.

Under the given assumptions, the reconverging transitions of coherence Def.
12
(2) are obtained directly from coherence of
Q
, by induction. The case that both transitions are from
R
by rule
(\textit{Sum}_{2})
is symmetrical. Finally, the immediate derivatives
P_{1}
and
P_{2}
of
P_{1}+P_{2}
are coherent for
\pi
, because they are coherent for
\pi_{i}
by assumption and thus also for the common extension
\pi_{i}\subseteq\pi
.

D.3
Parallel

Lemma D.82

(Key lemma)
.

Let
Q\Vdash\pi
and
R\Vdash\pi
be such that
\pi\,\backslash\,\mathcal{R}
is a pivot policy. Then
(Q\,|\,R)\Vdash\pi
.

Proof D.83
.

Let
P=Q\,|\,R
such that both
Q
and
R
are coherent for
\pi
where
\pi\,\backslash\,\mathcal{R}
is a pivot policy.
For conformance consider a transition

P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{{}_{H}}\,$}P^{\prime}

with
\ell\in\mathcal{L}
and
\ell^{\prime}\in H
. First suppose
\ell\not\in\mathcal{C}
. Then this transition is not a synchronisation but a transition of either one of the sub-processes
Q
by
(\textit{Par}_{1})
or
P
by
(\textit{Par}_{2})
with the same blocking set
H
. In either case, we thus use coherence
Q\Vdash\pi
or
R\Vdash\pi
to conclude
\ell^{\prime}\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\;\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}{}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip-6.75003pt\raisebox{6.22778pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt\scriptsize$$\hskip 3.00003pt}\hskip 0.74997pt\raisebox{0.22223pt}{\scalebox{.5}[.5]{$\succ$}}\;$}\ell\in\pi
.
If
\ell=\sigma
, then the transition in question is a synchronisation of
Q
and
R
via rule
(\textit{Com})

Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}

with
E\equiv{{\mathsf{0}}}
,
H=H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2}
and
P^{\prime}=Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}
generated from transitions

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}\text{ and }R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}}}\,$}R_{2}.

and
B_{2}=\{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}\mid H_{2}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}(R)\not\subseteq\{\sigma\}\text{ or }N_{2}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}(Q)\not\subseteq\{\sigma\}\}
.
By Lem.
C.56
we have
B_{2}=\{\,\}
. If we now take an arbitrary
\ell^{\prime}\in H\cap\mathcal{L}
then
\ell^{\prime}\in H_{2}
or
\ell^{\prime}\in N_{2}
. In these cases, we obtain
\ell^{\prime}\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\;\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}{}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip-6.75003pt\raisebox{6.22778pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt\scriptsize$$\hskip 3.00003pt}\hskip 0.74997pt\raisebox{0.22223pt}{\scalebox{.5}[.5]{$\succ$}}\;$}\ell\in\pi
by conformance Def.
17
from
Q\Vdash\pi
or
R\Vdash\pi
.

In the sequel, we tackle coherence Def.
12
.
As before, we work in the SOS without
(\textit{Struct})
but with symmetrical rules
(\textit{Par}_{i})
. First we observe that the immediate derivatives of
Q\,|\,R
are always parallel compositions
Q^{\prime}\,|\,R^{\prime}
of derivatives of
Q
and
R
. Thus, we may assume that the immediate derivatives
Q^{\prime}\,|\,R^{\prime}
are coherent for
\pi
because coherence of
P
and
Q
is preserved under transitions (i.e., by co-induction). Now suppose

P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}P_{1}\text{ and }P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}P_{2}

such that
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
or
P_{1}\not\equiv P_{2}
or
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha_{i}\not\in H_{3-i}
. Moreover, the c-actions
\alpha_{1}{:}H_{1}[E_{1}]
and
\alpha_{2}{:}H_{2}[E_{2}]
are interference-free.
We argue reconvergence by case analysis on the rules that generate these transitions. These could be
(\textit{Par}_{1})
,
(\textit{Par}_{2})
or
(\textit{Com})
.
First note that by Lem.
C.56
we can drop the consideration of the synchronisation action
\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}
for the blocking set in
(\textit{Com})
altogether:

P\,|\,Q\xrightarrow[R_{1}\,|\,R_{2}]{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}\cup H_{2}}}\,P^{\prime}\,|\,Q^{\prime}\lx@proof@logical@and P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{R_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}P^{\prime}Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{R_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}

We will use the name
(\textit{Com}_{a})
to refer to the instance of
(\textit{Com})
with
\ell\in\mathcal{R}
and the name
(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})
for the instance with
\ell\in\mathcal{C}
and we proceeds by case analysis.

•

(\textit{Par}_{1},\textit{Par}_{2})\mathrel{\diamond}{}(\textit{Com}_{a})
. We start with the case where one of the reductions to
P_{i}
is non-synchronising by
(\textit{Par}_{1})
or
(\textit{Par}_{2})
and the second reduction to
P_{3-i}
is a synchronisation obtained by
(\textit{Com}_{a})
. By symmetry, it suffices to consider the case that the non-synchronising transition is by
(\textit{Par}_{1})
from
Q
, i.e.,
\alpha_{1}\not\in C
and
\alpha_{2}=\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}
and

Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}\,|\,R}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\,|\,R\text{ and }Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}\,|\,G_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime}\cup N_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}

with
E_{1}=F_{1}\,|\,R
,
E_{2}=F_{2}\,|\,G_{2}
,

H_{2}=H_{2}^{\prime}\cup N_{2}
,
P_{1}=Q_{1}\,|\,R
and
P_{2}=Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}
,
where
\ell\in\mathcal{R}
is a rendezvous action such that

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\text{ and }Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q_{2}\text{ and }R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{G_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}}}\,$}R_{2}.

We may safely assume
\alpha_{1}\neq\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}=\tau
or
Q_{1}\,|\,R\not\equiv Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}
. The third case
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
is excluded since
\alpha_{2}=\tau
. Further, suppose the c-actions
\alpha_{1}{:}H_{1}[F_{1}\,|\,R]
and
\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}{:}(H_{2}^{\prime}\cup N_{2})[F_{2}\,|\,G_{2}]
are non-interfering.
Observe that
\{\alpha_{1},\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}\}\not\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and also
\{\alpha_{1},\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}\}\cap\mathcal{C}=\{\,\}
. Thus only a weak environment shift is needed. We wish to exploit coherence of
Q
. The first observation is that since
\alpha_{2}=\tau
, the assumptions on non-interference Def.
11
(2) implies that
H_{1}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{1}\,|\,R)=\{\,\}
. Since
\ell=\overline{\overline{\ell}}\in\overline{\textsf{iA}}(R)\cap\mathcal{L}\subseteq\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{1}\,|\,R)
by Lem.
C.44
and Lem.
C.46
, this implies that
\ell\not\in H_{1}
up front.
Next, if
\alpha_{1}\neq\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}
, non-interference Def.
11
(1) means

\alpha_{1}\not\in H_{2}=H_{2}^{\prime}\cup N_{2}
and thus

\alpha_{1}\not\in H_{2}^{\prime}
.
The same is true if
\alpha_{1}=\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}=\tau
, but then by non-interference Def.
11
(2) we have the non-interference equation

(H_{2}^{\prime}\cup N_{2})\cap(\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{2}\,|\,G_{2})\cup\{\tau\})=H_{2}\cap(\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(E_{2})\cup\{\tau\})=\{\,\}

from which
\alpha_{1}=\tau\not\in H_{2}^{\prime}
follows.
This settles the first part of the non-interference property Def.
11
(1) for the diverging transitions out of
Q
.

For the second part of non-interference, suppose
\alpha_{1}=\tau
. Then the non-interference assumption
implies that
H_{2}^{\prime}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{2}\,|\,G_{2})=\{\,\}
.
Now since
\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{2})\subseteq\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{2}\,|\,G_{2})

we conclude from this that
H_{2}^{\prime}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{2})=\{\,\}
.
This completes the proof that the c-actions
\alpha_{1}{:}H_{1}[F_{1}]
and
\ell{:}H_{2}^{\prime}[F_{2}]
must be non-interfering in all cases.

Note that we always have
\alpha_{1}\neq\ell
or
\{\alpha_{1},\ell\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
, and in the latter case also
\alpha_{1}\not\in H_{2}^{\prime}
and
\ell\not\in H_{1}
from the above.
Hence, we can use coherence Def.
12
of
Q

and obtain processes
F_{1}^{\prime}
,
F_{2}^{\prime}
,
Q^{\prime}
with reconverging transitions

Q_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\text{ and }Q_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}

such that
H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\subseteq H_{2}^{\prime}
and
H_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq H_{1}
.
We now invoke
(\textit{Com}_{a})
and
(\textit{Par}_{1})
to obtain reconverging transitions

Q_{1}\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cup N_{2}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,|\,R_{2}\text{ and }Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}\,|\,R_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,|\,R_{2}

such that
H_{1}^{\prime}\subseteq H_{1}
and
H_{2}^{\prime\prime}\cup N_{2}\subseteq H_{2}^{\prime}\cup N_{2}
.
Regarding the concurrent environments, if
\alpha_{1}\in\mathcal{R}
then the coherence of
Q
guarantees that

F_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }F_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}
from which we infer

F_{2}\,|\,G_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{2}\text{ and }F_{1}\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\,|\,R_{2}.

If
\alpha_{1}=\tau
the above guarantee from the coherence of
Q
is weaker: We only have
F_{1}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ell}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{2}^{\prime}
.
In any case we obtain the environment shifts

F_{2}\,|\,G_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{2}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{2}\text{ and }F_{1}\,|\,R\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{1}^{\prime}\,|\,R_{2},
as required.

•

(\textit{Par}_{1})\mathrel{\diamond}{}(\textit{Par}_{2})
: Suppose the two non-interfering transitions of
P=Q\,|\,R
are by
(\textit{Par}_{1})
from
Q
and
(\textit{Par}_{2})
from
R
. Thus, we are looking at transitions

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\text{ and }R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}R_{2}

for
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}\cup\{\tau\}
combined via
(\textit{Par})
to generate

Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}\,|\,R}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\,|\,R\text{ and }Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{Q\,|\,F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q\,|\,R_{2}

with
E_{1}=F_{1}\,|\,R
and
E_{2}=Q\,|\,F_{2}
.
Then we can directly construct reconverging transitions, applying
(\textit{Par})
for

Q_{1}\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{Q_{1}\,|\,F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q_{1}\,|\,R_{2}\text{ and }Q\,|\,R_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}\,|\,R_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\,|\,R_{2}.

Obviously,
Q_{1}\,|\,R_{2}
reduces to
Q_{1}\,|\,R_{2}
and by
(\textit{Par}_{1})
and
(\textit{Par}_{2})

we also have the strong environment shifts

F_{1}\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}

F_{1}\,|\,R_{2}

\text{and }Q\,|\,F_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}Q_{1}\,|\,F_{2}
which completes the claim for we have, in particular,
F_{1}\,|\,R\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{2}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{1}\,|\,R_{2}
and
Q\,|\,F_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}Q_{1}\,|\,F_{2}
.
Notice that in this case we do not need to assume that
Q
or
R
are coherent, i.e., the assumption that the c-actions
\alpha_{i}{:}H_{i}[E_{i}]
are interference-free.

•

(\textit{Par}_{i})\mathrel{\diamond}{}(\textit{Par}_{i})
for
i\in\{1,2\}
: Suppose both non-interfering and diverging reductions are by
(\textit{Par}_{1})
from
Q
(or symmetrically, both by
(\textit{Par}_{2})
from
R
). Then,

Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}\,|\,R}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\,|\,R\text{ and }Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}\,|\,R}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}\,|\,R

with
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}\cup\{\tau\}
and

P_{i}=Q_{i}\,|\,R
,
E_{i}=F_{i}\,|\,R
, where

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\text{ and }Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}

with
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
or
Q_{1}\,|\,R\not\equiv Q_{2}\,|\,R
or
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha_{i}\not\in H_{3-i}
. This, in particular, implies
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
.
Further, assume
non-interference of
\alpha_{i}{:}H_{i}[F_{i}\,|\,R]
.
We obtain non-interference of
\alpha_{i}{:}H_{i}[F_{i}]
by Prop.
C.48
.
We can therefore apply coherence Def.
12
of
Q
and obtain processes
F_{1}^{\prime}
,
F_{2}^{\prime}
and
Q^{\prime}
with reconverging transitions

Q_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\text{ and }Q_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}

with
H_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq H_{i}
and such that

\displaystyle F_{1}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{2}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }F_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{2}^{\prime}

(2)

and if
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
or
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
, more strongly

\displaystyle F_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }F_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}.

(3)

Reapplying
(\textit{Par}_{1})
we construct the transitions

Q_{1}\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}\,|\,R}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,|\,R\text{ and }Q_{2}\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}\,|\,R}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,|\,R.

Obviously, by
(\textit{Par}_{1})
and
(\textit{Par}_{2})
we also obtain transitions

F_{1}\,|\,R\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{2}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{1}^{\prime}\,|\,R

and

F_{2}\,|\,R\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{2}^{\prime}\,|\,R

from (
2
) or, more strongly,

F_{1}\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\,|\,R

and

F_{2}\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}\,|\,R

from (
3
) if
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
or
Q_{1}\,|\,R\not\equiv Q_{2}\,|\,R
.

•

(\textit{Com}_{a})\mathrel{\diamond}{}(\textit{Com}_{a})
:
This is the most interesting case in which we are going to exploit the assumption that
Q
and
R
are coherent for the same pivot policy
\pi
. Without loss of generality we assume
P=Q\,|\,R
and both the reductions

Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{i}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{i}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{i}\cup N_{i}}}\,$}Q_{i}\,|\,R_{i}

with
P_{i}=Q_{i}\,|\,R_{i}
are
\tau
-actions generated by the communication rule
(\textit{Com})
, i.e.
\alpha_{1}=\ell_{1}\,|\,\overline{\ell_{1}}=\tau=\ell_{2}\,|\,\overline{\ell_{2}}=\alpha_{2}
for actions
\ell_{i}\in\mathcal{R}
. Since
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}
and
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}=\{\tau\}\not\subseteq\mathcal{R}
we only need to prove confluence the case that
P_{1}\not\equiv P_{2}
, i.e.,
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
or
R_{1}\not\equiv R_{2}
. Moreover, we only need a weak environment shift, because
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}=\{\tau\}\not\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\cap\mathcal{C}=\{\,\}
.
Also, non-interference Def.
11
(2) means that
(H_{i}\cup N_{i})\cap(\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(E_{i})\cup\{\tau\})=\{\,\}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle H_{i}\cap(\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(E_{i})\cup\{\tau\})=\{\,\}\text{ and }N_{i}\cap(\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(E_{i})\cup\{\tau\})=\{\,\}.

(4)

Thus, we are looking at transitions

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{i}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell_{i}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{i}}}\,$}Q_{i}\text{ and }R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{G_{i}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}_{i}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{i}}}\,$}R_{i}.

We claim that the two c-actions
\ell_{i}{:}H_{i}[F_{i}]
of
Q
, and likewise the two c-actions
\overline{\ell}_{i}{:}N_{i}[G_{i}]
of
R
, are interference-free.
Note that since
\ell_{i}\neq\tau
, we only need to consider the first part of Def.
11
(1).
We first show that at least one of these pairs of c-actions must be interference free, because of coherence and conformance to
\pi
where
\pi\,\backslash\,\mathcal{R}
is a pivot policy. It will then transpire that the other must be interference-free, too, because of the assumption (
4
).

Obviously, if
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}
then both
\ell_{i}{:}H_{i}[F_{i}]
of
Q
and
\overline{\ell}_{i}{:}N_{i}[G_{i}]
are interference-free for trivial reasons.
We distinguish two cases depending on whether
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
are identical or not. So, suppose
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
. Both
Q
and
R
are coherent for the same policy
\pi
and
\pi\,\backslash\,\mathcal{R}
is a pivot policy. Thus,
\ell_{1}\mathrel{\diamond}{}\ell_{2}\in\pi
or
\overline{\ell}_{1}\mathrel{\diamond}{}\overline{\ell}_{2}\in\pi
, by Def.
C.51
.
Hence,
\ell_{i}\not\in H_{3-i}
or
\overline{\ell}_{i}\not\in N_{3-i}
by conformance Def.
17
, which means that at least one of the pairs of c-actions
\ell_{i}{:}H_{i}[F_{i}]
of
Q
or the two c-actions
\overline{\ell}_{i}{:}N_{i}[G_{i}]
must be interference-free by policy.

Now we argue that if
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
and one of the pairs
\ell_{i}{:}H_{i}[F_{i}]
or
\overline{\ell}_{i}{:}N_{i}[G_{i}]
is interference-free the other must be, too, and so we can close the diamond for both
Q
and
R
. By symmetry, it suffices to run the argument in case that the c-actions
\ell_{i}{:}H_{i}[F_{i}]
of
Q
are non-interfering. Then, we have
\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\ell_{i}\not\in H_{3-i}
.
Hence, we invoke coherence Def.
12
of
Q
obtaining processes
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
with transitions (strong environment shift)

F_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell_{2}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }F_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}
and a process
Q^{\prime}
with transitions

Q_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\text{ and }Q_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}.

such that
H_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq H_{i}
.
This means
\ell_{i}\in\textsf{iA}(F_{3-i})\subseteq\textsf{iA}(F_{3-i}\,|\,G_{3-i})=\textsf{iA}(E_{3-i})\subseteq\textsf{iA}^{\ast}(E_{3-i})
and therefore
\overline{\ell}_{i}\not\in N_{3-i}
because of (
4
). Thus, as claimed, the c-actions
\overline{\ell}_{i}{:}N_{i}[G_{i}]
are interference-free, too. Again
\{\overline{\ell}_{1},\overline{\ell}_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\overline{\ell}_{i}\not\in N_{3-i}
entitles us to exploit coherence Def.
12
for
R

from which we obtain processes
G_{1}^{\prime}
and
G_{2}^{\prime}
with transitions
(again, a strong environment shift)

G_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}_{2}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}G_{1}^{\prime}\text{ as well as }G_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}G_{2}^{\prime}
as well as a process
R^{\prime}
with

R_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{G_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}R^{\prime}\text{ and }R_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{G_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}R^{\prime}.

with
N_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq N_{i}
.
Finally, we invoke
(\textit{Com})
to obtain the re-converging reductions

Q_{1}\,|\,R_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell_{2}\,|\,\overline{\ell_{2}}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime}\cup N_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,|\,R^{\prime}\text{ and }Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell_{1}\,|\,\overline{\ell_{1}}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime}\cup N_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,|\,R^{\prime}

with
H_{i}^{\prime}\cup N_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq H_{i}\cup N_{i}
,
and also

F_{i}\,|\,G_{i}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell_{3-i}\,|\,\overline{\ell_{3-i}}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{i}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{i}^{\prime}

as required, since this implies the weaker form

F_{i}\,|\,G_{i}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{i}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{i}^{\prime}
of environment shift.

It remains to consider the case that
\ell_{1}=\ell=\ell_{2}
. By assumption
P_{1}\not\equiv P_{2}
and so we must have
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
or
R_{1}\not\equiv R_{2}
. Suppose
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
. If
R_{1}\not\equiv R_{2}
we apply a symmetric argument with the role of
\ell_{i}
and
\overline{\ell}_{i}
interchanged.
As observed above, the c-actions
\ell_{i}{:}H_{i}[F_{i}]
are trivially interference-free according to Def.
11
, because
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{i}\neq\tau
.
Then, by coherence Def.
12
applied to
Q
we conclude that there are processes
F_{1}^{\prime}
and
F_{2}^{\prime}
with transitions

F_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }F_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}
and a process
Q^{\prime}
with transitions (the strong environment shift arises here because we have
\ell\in\mathcal{R}
and
Q_{1}\nequiv Q_{2}
)

Q_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\text{ and }Q_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}.

such that
H_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq H_{i}
. This means that
\ell\in\textsf{iA}(F_{3-i})\subseteq\textsf{iA}(E_{3-i})
and so
\overline{\ell}\not\in N_{3-i}
because of
N_{3-i}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(E_{3-i})=\{\,\}
as per (
4
).
If
R_{1}\equiv R_{2}
then for each
i\in\{1,2\}
, by coherence Def.
12
(as
\overline{\ell}\not\in N_{3-i}\cup\mathcal{C}
) there is a transition

R_{i}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{G_{3-i}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{3-i}^{\prime}}}\,$}R^{\prime}

with
N_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq N_{i}
and
G_{i}\equiv G_{i}^{\prime}
or
G_{i}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}G_{i}^{\prime}
. Using these transitions we now recombine

Q_{i}\,|\,R_{i}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{3-i}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{3-i}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{3-i}^{\prime}\cup N_{3-i}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,|\,R^{\prime}

with
H_{i}^{\prime}\cup N_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq H_{i}\cup N_{i}
. Note that if
G_{i}\equiv G_{i}^{\prime}
then

F_{i}\,|\,G_{i}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{i}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{i}=F_{i}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{i}^{\prime}

or if
G_{i}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}G_{i}^{\prime}
then

F_{i}\,|\,G_{i}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{i}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{i}^{\prime}.

This means that, in all cases,
F_{i}\,|\,G_{i}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{i}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{i}^{\prime}
as desired. If
R_{1}\not\equiv R_{2}
we can apply coherence Def
12
to
R
in the same way and close the diamond with
processes
G_{1}^{\prime}
and
G_{2}^{\prime}
and transitions (again, the strong environment shift arises here because we have
\overline{\ell}\in\mathcal{R}
and
R_{1}\nequiv R_{2}
)

G_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}G_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }G_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}G_{2}^{\prime}
as well as a process
R^{\prime}
with

R_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{G_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}R^{\prime}\text{ and }R_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{G_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}R^{\prime}.

with
N_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq N_{i}
. Finally, we invoke
(\textit{Com})
to obtain the re-converging reductions

Q_{1}\,|\,R_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime}\cup N_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,|\,R^{\prime}\text{ and }Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime}\cup N_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,|\,R^{\prime}

with
H_{i}^{\prime}\cup N_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq H_{i}\cup N_{i}
,
and also

F_{i}\,|\,G_{i}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{i}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{i}^{\prime}
which also implies
F_{i}\,|\,G_{i}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{i}^{\prime}\,|\,G_{i}^{\prime}

as required.

•

(\textit{Com}_{a})\mathrel{\diamond}{}(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})
and, by symmetry, the induction case
(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})\mathrel{\diamond}{}(\textit{Com}_{a})
: We assume
P=Q\,|\,R
and the non-interfering, diverging reductions

Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}\cup N_{1}\cup B_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\,|\,R_{1}\text{ and }Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}

for
\ell\in\mathcal{L}
,
\sigma\in\mathcal{C}
, with
P_{i}=Q_{i}\,|\,R_{i}
and
E_{i}=F_{i}\,|\,G_{i}
are generated by instances
(\textit{Com}_{a})
and
(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})
of the communication rules, respectively. These transitions arise from

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\text{ and }R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{G_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\overline{\ell}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{1}}}\,$}R_{1}\text{ and }Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}\text{ and }R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}}}\,$}R_{2}.

We assume that the c-actions
(\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}){:}(H_{1}\cup N_{1}\cup B_{1})[E_{1}]
and
\sigma{:}(H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2})[{{\mathsf{0}}}]
are non-interfering.
Note that
\ell\neq\sigma
and
\overline{\ell}\neq\sigma
.
First, observe that by Lem.
C.56
,
B_{1}=\{\,\}=B_{2}
.
Also,
\ell\,|\,\overline{\ell}=\tau\neq\sigma
and so the non-interference assumption means that
\sigma\not\in H_{1}\cup N_{1}\cup B_{1}
as well as
\tau\not\in H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2}
. The other part of the non-interference, from Def.
11
(1), does not provide any extra information.

We claim that
\ell\in H_{2}
. By contradiction, if
\ell\not\in H_{2}
then, since
\sigma\not\in H_{1}
, the two c-actions
\ell{:}H_{1}[F_{1}]
and
\sigma{:}H_{2}[{{\mathsf{0}}}]
of
Q
would be interference-free according to Def.
11
. But then, since
\{\ell,\sigma\}\cap\mathcal{C}\neq\{\,\}
, coherence Def.
12
applied to
Q
would imply

\sigma\in\textsf{iA}(F_{1})
and
\ell\in\textsf{iA}({{\mathsf{0}}})
. The latter, however, is impossible. Thus, as claimed,
\ell\in H_{2}
. Likewise, from
\sigma\not\in N_{1}
we derive
\overline{\ell}\in N_{2}
in the same fashion.

Now we invoke the fact that
\tau\not\in B_{2}
, i.e.

H_{2}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}(R)\subseteq\{\sigma\}
and
N_{2}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}(Q)\subseteq\{\sigma\}
. But
\overline{\ell}\in\textsf{iA}(R)
and
\ell\in\textsf{iA}(Q)
, whence we must have
\ell\not\in H_{2}
and
\overline{\ell}\not\in N_{2}
. This is a contradiction.
Hence, transitions obtained by rules
(\textit{Com}_{a})
and
(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})
are never interference-free.

•

\{(\textit{Par}_{1}),(\textit{Par}_{2})\}\mathrel{\diamond}{}(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})
:
As the representative case, we assume
P=Q\,|\,R
and the non-interfering, diverging reductions are

Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}\,|\,R}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\,|\,R\text{ and }Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}

with
\alpha_{1}\in\mathcal{R}\cup\{\tau\}
so that
P_{1}\equiv Q_{1}\,|\,R
and
P_{2}\equiv Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}
are generated by the rule
(\textit{Par}_{1})
and
(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})
, respectively, from transitions

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\text{ and }Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}\text{ and }R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}}}\,$}R_{2}.

where
\alpha_{1}\neq\sigma
and the c-actions
\alpha_{1}{:}H_{1}[F_{1}\,|\,R]
and
\sigma{:}(H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2})[{{\mathsf{0}}}]
are interference-free. First, Def.
11
(1) implies
\alpha_{1}\not\in H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2}
and
\sigma\not\in H_{1}
. Further, if
\alpha_{1}=\tau
then

(H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2})\cap(\textsf{iA}^{\ast}({{\mathsf{0}}})\cup\{\tau\})=\{\,\}
, which in particular means
H_{2}\cap(\textsf{iA}^{\ast}({{\mathsf{0}}})\cup\{\tau\})=\{\,\}
. Therefore, the c-actions
\alpha_{1}{:}H_{1}[F_{1}]
and
\sigma{:}H_{2}[{{\mathsf{0}}}]
are interference-free. Note that
\{\alpha_{1},\sigma\}\cap\mathcal{C}\neq\{\,\}
. Hence we can exploit coherence Def.
12
for
Q
, obtaining the stronger form of environment shift. But this would imply
\alpha_{1}\in\textsf{iA}({{\mathsf{0}}})
which is impossible.
So we find that transitions obtained by rules
(\textit{Par}_{i})
and
(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})
are never interference-free in the sense of coherence.

•

(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})\mathrel{\diamond}{}(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})
:
We assume
P=Q\,|\,R
and the non-interfering, diverging reductions

Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}\cup N_{1}\cup B_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\,|\,R_{1}\text{ and }Q\,|\,R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}\cup N_{2}\cup B_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}

with
P_{i}=Q_{i}\,|\,R_{i}
and
E_{i}=F_{i}\,|\,G_{i}
are generated by the communication rules
(\textit{Com}_{\sigma})
. Observing that
\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
is impossible, here, we may assume
\sigma_{1}\neq\sigma_{2}
or
Q_{1}\,|\,R_{1}\nequiv Q_{2}\,|\,R_{2}
. This can only hold true if
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
or
R_{1}\not\equiv R_{2}
. Furthermore, the two clock transitions of
Q_{1}\,|\,Q_{2}
can be assumed to be interference-free.
From this it follows that the two underlying clock transitions

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}

and

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}

and the likewise the transitions

R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{1}}}\,$}R_{1}

and

R\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{{{\mathsf{0}}}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}}}\,$}R_{2}

are interference-free. If
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
then we could apply coherence of
Q
and obtain
\sigma\in\textsf{iA}({{\mathsf{0}}})
, if
R_{1}\not\equiv R_{2}
the same follows from coherence of
R
. This is impossible, whence the case can be excluded.

D.4
Hiding

Proposition D.84
.

If
Q
is coherent for
\pi
and
\sigma\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\;\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}{}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip-6.75003pt\raisebox{6.22778pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt\scriptsize$$\hskip 3.00003pt}\hskip 0.74997pt\raisebox{0.22223pt}{\scalebox{.5}[.5]{$\succ$}}\;$}\ell\not\in\pi
for all
\sigma\in L
and
\ell\in\pi
, then
Q\,/\penalty\,L
is coherent for
\pi
.

Proof D.85
.

It suffices to prove the proposition for the special case
Q\,/\penalty\,\sigma
of a single clock. We recall the semantic rule for this case:

\begin{array}[]{l@{\hspace{2cm}}l}\lx@intercol P\,/\penalty\,\sigma\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H^{\prime}}}\,$}Q\,/\penalty\,\sigma\lx@proof@logical@and P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha}$}}\!\!{{}_{H}}\,$}QH^{\prime}=H-\{\sigma\}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

where
\sigma\,/\penalty\,\sigma=\tau
and
\alpha\,/\penalty\,\sigma=\alpha
if
\alpha\neq\sigma
.
Let
P=Q\,/\penalty\,\sigma
and the two non-interfering and diverging transitions

P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}P_{1}\text{ and }P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,$}P_{2}

arise by
(\textit{Hide})
from transitions

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}\text{ and }Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}}}\,$}Q_{2}

with
E_{i}=F_{i}\,/\penalty\,\sigma
,
P_{i}=Q_{i}\,/\penalty\,\sigma
and
H_{i}=N_{i}-\{\sigma\}
.
For coherence Def.
12
we assume that
\alpha_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\neq\alpha_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma
or
P_{1}\not\equiv P_{2}
, or
\{\alpha_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma,\alpha_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha_{i}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\not\in H_{3-i}
. Further, let the c-actions
\alpha_{i}{:}H_{i}[E_{i}]
be interference-free. Observe that the first case implies
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
and the second case means
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
. Moreover, because of the assumption that
\sigma\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\;\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}{}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip-6.75003pt\raisebox{6.22778pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt\scriptsize$$\hskip 3.00003pt}\hskip 0.74997pt\raisebox{0.22223pt}{\scalebox{.5}[.5]{$\succ$}}\;$}\alpha_{i}\not\in\pi
we must have
\sigma\not\in N_{1}\cup N_{2}
. But this means
H_{i}=N_{i}
.

First, suppose that
\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}=\sigma
. But then the assumption
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
contradicts strong determinacy of clocks (Proposition
15
). Thus, the actions
\alpha_{i}
cannot both be the clock
\sigma
that is hidden. The case
\alpha_{i}=\sigma
and
\alpha_{3-i}\in\mathit{Act}-\{\sigma\}
, for some fixed
i\in\{1,2\}
can also be excluded as follows: By Prop.
D.61
the clock transition and the non-clock transition must interfere in
Q
, i.e.,
\alpha_{i}\in N_{3-i}
or
\alpha_{3-i}\in N_{i}
. The former
\sigma=\alpha_{i}\in N_{3-i}
is impossible because of the above. The latter is outright impossible since then
\alpha_{3-i}\,/\penalty\,\sigma=\alpha_{3-i}\in N_{i}=H_{i}
which contradicts the non-interference assumption on the transitions of
P
.

The only remaining case to handle is that both of the diverging transitions are by labels
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathit{Act}-\{\sigma\}
and
\alpha_{i}\,/\penalty\,\sigma=\alpha_{i}
for both
i\in\{1,2\}
. We claim that the c-actions
\alpha_{i}{:}N_{i}[F_{i}]
are interference-free. If
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
then non-interference assumption directly gives
\alpha_{i}\not\in H_{i}=N_{i}
. Next, if for some
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{i}\,/\penalty\,\sigma=\tau
, then the assumptions on
P
not only give
\tau\not\in H_{3-i}=N_{3-i}
but also

N_{3-i}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{3-i}\,/\penalty\,\sigma)=H_{3-i}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(E_{3-i})=\{\,\}.

Since by assumption on policy conformance
\sigma\not\in N_{3-i}
the latter implies
N_{3-i}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{3-i})=\{\,\}
. Now we have shown that the diverging transitions
of
Q
are non-interfering we can use the coherence Def.
12
for
Q
by induction hypothesis and get
F_{1}^{\prime}
,
F_{2}^{\prime}
,
Q^{\prime}
such that

Q_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\text{ and }Q_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\text{ with }F_{1}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{2}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }F_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{2}^{\prime}

such that
N_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq N_{i}
. By applying
(\textit{Hide})
to these transitions, we obtain

P_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\text{ and }P_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,/\penalty\,\sigma

where
H_{i}^{\prime\prime}=N_{i}^{\prime}-\{\sigma\}\subseteq N_{i}-\{\sigma\}=H_{i}
. Note, by rule
(\textit{Hide})
we also have

F_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{1}^{\prime}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\text{ and }F_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{2}^{\prime}\,/\penalty\,\sigma.

In the special case that
\{\alpha_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma,\alpha_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\}=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
or
Q_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\not\equiv Q_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma
, or
\{\alpha_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma,\alpha_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\}\cap\mathcal{C}=\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\cap\mathcal{C}\neq\{\,\}
we obtain the stronger context shifts

F_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }F_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}
from which we infer

F_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\text{ and }F_{2}\,/\penalty\,\sigma\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}\,/\penalty\,\sigma}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}\,/\penalty\,\sigma.

This completes the proof.

D.5
Restriction

Let us next look at the restriction operator.
The following definition is relevant for creating coherent processes under action restriction (App.
D.5
).

The fact that
P
is locally coherent does not imply that
P\,\backslash\,L
is locally coherent. Consider the process
Q=(s+a{:}s\,|\,b{\mathsf{.}}\overline{s})\,\backslash\,s
which is conformant and pivotable. The signal
s
blocks the action
a
by priority in the thread
s+a{:}s
. For the communication partner
\overline{s}
to become active, however, it needs the synchronisation with an external action
\overline{b}
. The transition

Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-1.99306pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{({{\mathsf{1}}}\,|\,b{\mathsf{.}}\overline{s})\,\backslash\,s}$}]{\raisebox{-3.98611pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{a}$}}\!\!{{}_{\{\,\}}}\,$}(b{\mathsf{.}}\overline{s})\,\backslash\,s

generated by
(\textit{Restr})
does not provide enough information in the blocking set
H
and environment
E
for us to be able to characterise the environments in which the action
a
is blocked. For instance, the parallel composition
Q\,|\,\overline{b}
will internally set the sender
\overline{s}
free and thus block the
a
-transition. From the above transition
the
(\textit{Com})
rule permits
Q\,|\,\overline{b}
to offer the
a
-step. This is not right as it is internally blocked and does not commute with the
b\,|\,\overline{b}
reduction. A simple solution to avoid such problems and preserve confluence is to force restricted signals so they do not block any visible actions.
This restriction suffices in many cases. Another, more general solution is to use the extended rule
\textit{Restr}^{*}
discussed above after Example
B.34
.

Proposition D.86
.

If
Q\Vdash\pi
and
\pi
is precedence-closed for
L\cup\overline{L}
, then
({Q}\,\backslash\,{L})\Vdash\pi\,\backslash\,L
.

Proof D.87
.

It suffices to prove the proposition for the special case
P=Q\,\backslash\,a
for a single channel name
a\in A
. Recall the rule
(\textit{Restr})
for this case:

\begin{array}[]{l@{\hspace{2cm}}l}\lx@intercol Q\,\backslash\,a\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E\,\backslash\,a}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha}$}}\!\!{{}_{H^{\prime}}}\,$}R\,\backslash\,a\lx@proof@logical@and Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha}$}}\!\!{{}_{H}}\,$}R\alpha\not\in\{a,\overline{a}\}H^{\prime}=H-\{a,\overline{a}\}\hfil\lx@intercol\end{array}

Consider a transition

Q\,\backslash\,a\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E\,\backslash\,a}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{{}_{H^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,\backslash\,a\text{ derived from }Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\ell}$}}\!\!{{}_{H}}\,$}Q^{\prime}

with
H^{\prime}=H-\{a,\overline{a}\}
,
\ell\not\in\{a,\overline{a}\}
and
\ell^{\prime}\in H^{\prime}
.
Then
\ell^{\prime}\not\in\{a,\overline{a}\}
and
\ell^{\prime}\in H
. Conformance Def.
17
applied to
Q
implies
\ell^{\prime}\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\;\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}{}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip-6.75003pt\raisebox{6.22778pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt\scriptsize$$\hskip 3.00003pt}\hskip 0.74997pt\raisebox{0.22223pt}{\scalebox{.5}[.5]{$\succ$}}\;$}\ell\in\pi
. But then
\ell^{\prime}\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\;\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}{}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip-6.75003pt\raisebox{6.22778pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt\scriptsize$$\hskip 3.00003pt}\hskip 0.74997pt\raisebox{0.22223pt}{\scalebox{.5}[.5]{$\succ$}}\;$}\ell\in\pi-a
, too, as both
\ell
and
\ell^{\prime}
a distinct from
a
and
\overline{a}
. This ensures conformance.

If
P=Q\,\backslash\,a
and the two non-interfering and diverging transitions

P_{2}\xleftarrow[\raisebox{-1.68887pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{2}}$}]{\alpha_{2}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}}}\,P\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}}}\,$}P_{1}

arise from rule
(\textit{Restr})
then we have diverging transitions

Q_{2}\xleftarrow[\raisebox{-1.68887pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}}$}]{\alpha_{2}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}}}\,Q\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{1}}}\,$}Q_{1}

with
H_{i}=N_{i}-\{a,\overline{a}\}
,
\alpha_{i}\not\in\{a,\overline{a}\}
, where
E_{i}\equiv{F_{i}}\,\backslash\,a
and
P_{i}\equiv{Q_{i}}\,\backslash\,a
. We assume
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
or
P_{1}\not\equiv P_{2}
or

\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha_{i}\not\in H_{3-i}
.
Further, suppose non-interference of
\alpha_{1}{:}H_{1}[E_{1}]
and
\alpha_{2}{:}H_{2}[E_{2}]
. Note that in case
P_{1}\not\equiv P_{2}
we immediately have
Q_{1}\not\equiv Q_{2}
. Next, notice that the premise of the
(\textit{Restr})
rule ensures
\alpha_{i}\not\in\{a,\overline{a}\}
. It is easy to see that this means
\{a,\overline{a}\}\cap N_{i}=\{\,\}
, i.e.,
H_{i}=N_{i}
. By contraposition, suppose
\ell\in\{a,\overline{a}\}
and
\ell\in N_{i}
. Then
\ell\raisebox{-1.0pt}{$\;\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}{}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip 1.5pt\raisebox{3.24445pt}{\rule{2.0pt}{0.4pt}}\hskip-6.75003pt\raisebox{6.22778pt}{\hskip 3.00003pt\scriptsize$$\hskip 3.00003pt}\hskip 0.74997pt\raisebox{0.22223pt}{\scalebox{.5}[.5]{$\succ$}}\;$}\alpha_{i}\in\pi
. But
\pi
is precedence-closed for
\{a,\overline{a}\}
, so we would have
\alpha_{i}\in\{a,\overline{a}\}
which is a contradiction. So,
H_{i}=N_{i}
.

All this means that to invoke coherence Def.
12
of the above
Q

is sufficent to show that the c-actions
\alpha_{1}{:}N_{1}[F_{1}]
and
\alpha_{2}{:}N_{2}[F_{2}]
do not interfere. Now, if
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
then from the non-interference assumption on the transitions out of
P
we get
\alpha_{i}\not\in H_{3-i}=N_{3-i}
. This is what we need for Def.
11
(1) to show that the c-actions
\alpha_{i}{:}N_{i}[F_{i}]
are interference-free.
For the second part Def.
11
(2) suppose
\alpha_{3-i}=\tau
for some
i\in\{1,2\}
. Then the assumed non-interference of the transitions out of
P
means
H_{i}\cap(\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(E_{i})\cup\{\tau\})=\{\,\}
, which is the same as
\tau\not\in H_{i}
and the following set condition

(N_{i}-\{a,\overline{a}\})\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{i}\,\backslash\,a)=\{\,\}.

Since
\{a,\overline{a}\}\cap N_{3-i}=\{\,\}
we have

N_{i}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{i})\subseteq\mathcal{L}-\{a,\overline{a}\}
.
We claim that the
\alpha_{i}
-transitions of
Q
is enabled, specifically
N_{i}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{i})=\{\,\}
observing that
\tau\not\in H_{i}=N_{i}
.
By contraposition, suppose
\beta\in N_{i}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{i})\subseteq\mathcal{L}-\{a,\overline{a}\}
. Hence,
\beta\not\in\{a,\overline{a}\}
and so
\beta\in N_{i}-\{a,\overline{a}\}
and also
\beta\in\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{i}\,\backslash\,a)
, but this contradicts the above set-condition.
Thus, we have shown that for all
i\in\{1,2\}
,
\alpha_{3-i}=\tau
implies
N_{i}\cap\overline{\textsf{iA}}^{{}_{\ast}}(F_{i})=\{\,\}
. This proves the c-actions
\alpha_{i}{:}N_{i}[F_{i}]
out of
Q
are interference-free. Hence, we can use coherence of
Q
for the diverging transition
and get
F_{1}^{\prime}
,
F_{2}^{\prime}
,
Q^{\prime}
such that

Q_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{2}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\text{ and }Q_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{F_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{N_{1}^{\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\text{ with }F_{1}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{2}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }F_{2}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{2}^{\prime}

such that
N_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq N_{i}
. We construct the required reconvergence by application of rule
(\textit{Restr})
:

P_{1}\mbox{\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{2}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{2}^{\prime\prime}}}\,$}}Q^{\prime}\,\backslash\,a\text{ and }P_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{E_{1}^{\prime}}$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{{}_{H_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}\,$}Q^{\prime}\,\backslash\,a\text{ with }F_{1}\,\backslash\,a\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{2}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{1}^{\prime}\,\backslash\,a\text{ and }F_{2}\,\backslash\,a\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\alpha_{1}}}{{\rightsquigarrow}}}F_{2}^{\prime}\,\backslash\,a

with
E_{i}^{\prime}=F_{i}^{\prime}\,\backslash\,a
and
H_{i}^{\prime\prime}=N_{i}^{\prime}-\{a,\overline{a}\}\subseteq N_{i}-\{a,\overline{a}\}=H_{i}
.
In the special case that
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\subseteq\mathcal{R}
and
\alpha_{1}\neq\alpha_{2}
or
P_{1}\not\equiv P_{2}
or
\{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\}\cap\mathcal{C}\neq\{\,\}
we can rely on the stronger context shifts

F_{1}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\text{ and }F_{2}\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}
to conclude

F_{1}\,\backslash\,a\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{2}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{1}^{\prime}\,\backslash\,a\text{ and }F_{2}\,\backslash\,a\mbox{ $\xrightarrow[\raisebox{-2.11113pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{$$}]{\raisebox{-4.22227pt}[0.0pt][0.0pt]{${}^{\alpha_{1}}$}}\!\!{}\,$}F_{2}^{\prime}\,\backslash\,a.
This completes the proof.
